deformation theory for ( ∞ , n ) (\infty,n) -categories
Roman Kositsyn

Abstract
. For an ( ∞ , n ) (\infty,n) -category ℰ \mathscr{E} we define an ( ∞ , 1 ) (\infty,1) category TwAr ⁡ ( ℰ ) \mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E}) and provide an isomorphism between the stabilization of the overcategory of ℰ \mathscr{E} in Cat ( ∞ , n ) \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)} and the ∞ \infty -category of spectrum-valued functors on TwAr ⁡ ( ℰ ) \mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E}) . We use this to develop the deformation theory of ( ∞ , n ) (\infty,n) -categories and apply it to given an ∞ \infty -categorical characterization of lax-idempotent monads.



1.
Introduction

Assume we have a (discrete)
n
-category
C
, then we can associate to it a chain complex

C_{n}\xrightarrow{\partial}C_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\partial}...\xrightarrow{\partial}C_{0}

of length
n
such that
C_{i}
is the factor of the free abelian group on the set of
i
-morphisms modulo relations

(1)

\displaystyle[f*_{k}g]=[f]+[g]

(2)

\displaystyle[\mathrm{id}_{t}]=0,

where
[f]
denotes the basis element corresponding to the morphism
f
,
*_{k}
for
k<i
denotes the operation of
k
-composition (so
f
and
g
in (
1
) are assumed to be
k
-composable) and
\mathrm{id}_{t}
is the identity
i
-morphism on some
j
-morphism
t
for
j<i
and
\partial[f]\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}[s(f)]-[t(f)]
, where
s
and
t
denote the source and target of the morphism respectively. This is easily seen to be an isomorphism invariant of
C
, however possibly not a very good one: denote by
\widetilde{C}
the groupoid obtained by inverting all
i
-morphisms in
C
for all
i
, then
C_{k}=\widetilde{C}_{k}
for all
k
: indeed, a general
i
-morphism in
\widetilde{C}
is a formal composition of morphisms in
C
and their inverses, note that by (
1
) we have

0=[\mathrm{id}_{s(f)}]=[f^{-1}*_{i}f]=[f^{-1}]+[f]\Rightarrow[f^{-1}]=[f],

which easily implies the claim.

This construction is the 1-categorical avatar of the stabilization construction from
\infty
-categories. More specifically, it is possible to show that
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Ch}_{n}(\mathrm{Sp})
, where
\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
is the
\infty
-category of
n
-fold Segal spaces and
\mathrm{Ch}_{n}(\mathrm{Sp})
denotes the (appropriately defined) stable
\infty
-category of chain complexes in spectra of length
n
. Given an
n
-fold Segal space
\mathscr{E}
(for example, an ordinary
n
-category) we can associate to it
\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathrm{Seg}}\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Ch}_{n}(\mathrm{Sp})
. If we consider the full subcategory
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
on
complete
Segal spaces, then its stabilization and given by
\mathrm{Sp}
and moreover the completion of a Segal space corresponds to the totalization of the corresponding complex. Just as in the discrete case, this construction only depends on the homotopy type of
\mathscr{E}
; in fact, we can say slightly more: the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ch}_{n}(\mathrm{Sp})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathrm{Seg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|-|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tot}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}

commutes up to homotopy, where the left vertical map is the functor of geometric realization and the bottom map associates to a space
X\in\mathscr{S}
its suspension spectrum.

Consider again the case of ordinary
n
-categories, but assume now that we have a functor
F:C\rightarrow D
to some ordinary
n
-category
D
that we will view as fixed. In that case for any
k
-morphism
f
in
D
we can consider the complex
C^{f}_{n}\xrightarrow{\partial}...\xrightarrow{\partial}C^{f}_{k}
where each terms is obtained by imposing relations (
1
) on the free abelian group on the set of morphisms lying over
f
. Note that for every
j
-composable pair
(f,g)
the operation of composition defines an operation

m:C_{i}^{f}\times C_{i}^{g}\rightarrow C_{i}^{F*_{j}g},

which endows
C^{f}_{i}
with the structure of the
local system of abelian groups
in the sense of
[
19
, Definition 3.5.10.]
. The
\infty
-categorical counterpart of this observation is
2.1
which identifies the stabilization of
\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}
for
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
with spectrum-values presheaves on
\Theta_{n,/\mathscr{E}}
that satisfy the Segal condition.

The bulk of this work is concerned with providing a simpler description of
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})
, before moving on to it let us first consider the results already present in the literature: the case
\dim(\mathscr{E})=1
was considered in
[
11
]
where it was shown that
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{E}})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E}),\mathrm{Sp})
, where
\mathrm{TwAr}
denotes the ordinary (
\infty
-categorical) twisted arrows category. The case
\dim(\mathscr{E})=2
was treated in
[
24
]
and is somewhat more difficult to present: in that case we also have
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{2,/\mathscr{E}})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{TwAr}_{2}(\mathscr{E}),\mathrm{Sp})
, where
\mathrm{TwAr}_{2}(\mathscr{E})
is an
\infty
-category that can be described informally as having objects given by 2-morphisms in
\mathscr{E}
and morphisms by geometric realizations of
\infty
-categories with objects

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

such that the source of the above morphism is
\beta
and the target is
\epsilon*_{1}(\alpha*_{)}\beta*_{\gamma})*_{1}\eta
, which we will denote by
(\epsilon|\alpha,\beta|\eta)
, and with morphisms given by isomorphisms
\alpha\cong\alpha^{\prime}_{1}*_{1}\alpha^{\prime}*_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{0}
and
\gamma\cong\gamma^{\prime}_{1}*_{1}\gamma^{\prime}*_{1}\gamma^{\prime}_{0}
such that the source of this morphism is given by
(\epsilon*_{1}(\alpha^{\prime}_{0}*_{0}b_{0}*_{0}\gamma^{\prime}_{0})|\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}|(\alpha^{\prime}_{1}*_{0}b_{1}*_{0}\gamma^{\prime}_{1})*_{1}\eta)
and the target by
(\epsilon|\alpha,\beta|\eta)
. The main result of the present work is

Theorem A
.

For
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
there is an isomorphism

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E}),\mathrm{Sp}),

where
\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E})
is the
\infty
-category described in
6.11
.

Unfortunately, the complexity of
\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E})
grows with dimension of
\mathscr{E}
, but we have given a rough description of it in
6.22
.

Observe that already in dimension 2 the diagram (
3
) defining a morphism in
\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E})
is not corepresentable by an object of
\Theta_{2}
. It is a more general form of pasting diagram, in order to handle those we use the formalism of Steiner complexes introduced (under a different name) in
[
25
]
. The actual definition of Steiner complexes is rather technical and reserved for
Section

3
, however the general idea is that (strong) Steiner complexes are complexes
C_{n}\xrightarrow{\partial}...\xrightarrow{\partial}C_{0}\xrightarrow{e}\mathbb{Z}
of free abelian groups
C_{i}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{\oplus m_{i}}
with augmentation
e
such that
e(b_{0})=1
for every basis element
b_{0}
on
C_{0}
satisfying an additional condition that the basis is ”strongly loop-free”. If the conditions are satisfied we can associate to it an
n
-category
C^{*}
such that the basis elements of
C_{i}
correspond to ”elementary
i
-cells” and every
k
-morphism in it can be uniquely presented as a composition of those elementary cells We use this formalism in order to ultimately define
\mathrm{TwAr}(\mathscr{E})
in
Section

6
, however we also obtain an important result concerning them:

Theorem B
.

Denote by
Stn_{n}
the category of strong Steiner complexes and by
x
its object, then considered as an
(\infty,n)
-category it is a free category on its elementary cells.

A more precise version of this statement can be found at the end of
Section

3
. Note that previously
B
has been proved for discrete
n
-categories in
[
2
]
, while an
\infty
-categorical version of it was also obtained in
[
6
]
.

The purpose for calculating the stabilization of the overcategories for us is deformation theory. A general formalism for deformation theories has been developed in
[
21
, Section 12]
, we will recall the basic definitions of it. First, given any presentable category
\mathscr{C}
, a morphism
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{C}
, an object
M\in\mathrm{Stab}(\mathscr{C}_{/y})
and
g^{\prime}:\Sigma^{\infty}x\rightarrow M
we can consider the pullback

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
g
is obtained from
g^{\prime}
using the adjunction
\Sigma^{\infty}\dashV\Omega^{\infty}
and the right vertical arrow is the zero section. We will call such an object
x^{\prime}
a
small extension
of
x
and we will call the objects of
\mathscr{C}_{/y}
that can be obtained by iterated small extensions from the terminal object
\mathrm{id}_{y}

Artinian objects
. The category
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
is well-suited for deformation theory since by the main result of
[
12
]
every object
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
can be viewed as an Artinian object of
\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{E}}
, where
\theta_{\leq n+1}:\mathrm{Cat}_{n}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}
is the left adjoint to the inclusion of
(n+1,n)
-categories into
(\infty,n)
-categories. Formal deformation theory then allows us to reduce the study of objects in
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
to the study of discrete categories in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}
and their deformation theory. More specifically, denote
L_{\mathscr{E}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\Sigma^{\infty}(*)\in\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})
, with this notation we have

Theorem C
.

Assume that
f:\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
is such that

\mathrm{CoKer}(f_{!}L_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow L_{\mathscr{D}})\cong 0

and
\tau_{\leq n+1}f
induces a monomorphism

\tau_{\leq n+1}f^{*}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}}(\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{D},A)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}}(\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{E},A)

for any
A\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}
, then
f^{*}
is also a monomorphism and moreover we have a pullback square

(4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{n}}(\mathscr{D},\mathscr{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}}(\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{D},\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{n}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{(n+1,n)}}(\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{E},\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathscr{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq n+1}f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq n+1}}

for any
\mathscr{A}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
.

Finally, in the last section we provide an application of this theorem: we denote by
\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}
the tricategory associated to the monoidal bicategory
\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}
which is a faithful subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}
containing finite ordinals
[m]
and endpoint-preserving functors between them. There is a natural morphism
\mathscr{I}:\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}
from the underlying bicategory of
\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}
which is a ”walking comonad”. The tricategory
\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}
is supposed to be the ”walking lax-idempotent comonad”, and we prove it in the last section using the deformation theory of
C
. More specifically, we prove

Theorem D
.

For
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{3}
the space of morphisms
F:\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{lax}}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}
is isomorphic to the subspace of
\mathrm{B}\Delta^{\mathrm{act}}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}
for which the image of the 2-morphism
\delta^{2}_{1}:[1]\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}[2]
is left adjoint to
\sigma_{0}^{1}:[2]\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}[1]
.

Notation and conventions.

In this paper we will call
(\infty,n)
-categories simply
n
-categories, whenever a certain result only holds for ordinary (discrete)
n
-categories we will specifically mention it. We will use
[
14
]
to identify
n
-categories with
n
-fold Segal spaces, we will denote by
\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
the category of
n
-categories and by
\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
the category of
n
-fold Segal spaces. We will use the term ”space” as a synonym for
\infty
-groupoid and denote by
\mathscr{S}
the category of spaces. For
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{D}
in
\mathrm{Cat}
we will denote
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{D}}(\mathscr{C})\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{D})
, so in particular the ordinary category of presheaves is denoted as
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\mathscr{C})
. We will denote by
\Theta_{n}
the disk category of
[
13
]
and identify
\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
with a subcategory of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta_{n})
on functors satisfying the Segal condition. Finally, we will make light use of basic notions and some basic results of
[
7
]
on algebraic patterns without further mention.

2.
Stabilization of
\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta}

Our goal in this section is to calculate the stabilization of
\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta}
for
\theta\in\Theta_{n}
. This is important since by
Lemma

2.3
we can (at least in theory) express
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})
for any
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
in terms of
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})
, and this property will be used numerous times in what follows to provide a more explicit definition of this stabilization.

Our argument will consist of two parts – first we will use the Dold-Kan correspondence for
\Theta_{n,/\theta}
explored in the appendix to identify
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})
with
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})
and then further employ the Verdier duality of
[
1
]
to identify this latter category with
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta})
.

Proposition 2.1
.

For an algebraic pattern
\mathscr{O}
the stabilization of
\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathscr{O}}(\mathscr{S}_{*})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathscr{O}}(\mathrm{Sp})
.

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathscr{O}}(\mathscr{S}))\cong\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathscr{O}}(\mathrm{Sp})

Construction 2.2
.

It follows from
2.1
that for
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
the stabilization of
\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}
can be identified with the category of functors
\Theta^{\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
satisfying the Segal condition, here we will introduce a stratification
T_{k}
of that category.

For
k\leq n
denote by
T_{\leq k}(\mathscr{E})
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathpzc{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}}(\mathrm{Sp})
on functors
\mathscr{F}
for which
\mathscr{F}(c_{p}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})\cong 0
for
p>k
, this is a full subcategory and the forgetful functor from
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})
to
T_{\leq k}(\mathscr{E})
preserves limits and colimits, hence
T_{\leq k}(\mathscr{E})
defines a stratification.

Lemma 2.3
.

For
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
there is an equivalence

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\lim}\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta}).

Assuming
\mathscr{E}
is complete, we also have an equivalence

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}})\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\lim}\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta}).

Proof.

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}
the left fibration over
\Theta_{n}
corresponding to
\mathscr{E}
, then
\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}
is a subcategory of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\widetilde{\mathscr{E}})
on functors satisfying the Segal condition. Note that
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\mathrm{colim}}\theta
, so

\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\widetilde{\mathscr{E}})\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\lim}\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\widetilde{\theta}).

This equivalence preserves the Segal condition and so defines an isomorphism

(5)

\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\lim}\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta}.

Note that the limit in (
5
) is taken over pullback morphisms
g^{*}:\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta^{\prime}}
for
g:\theta\rightarrow\theta^{\prime}
, they are right adjoint functors (with left adjoint
g_{!}
) between presentable categories, so (
5
) can be viewed as a colimit diagram in
\mathrm{PrCat}
. The claim now follows since stabilization is given by tensoring with
\mathrm{Sp}
in
\mathrm{PrCat}
and the monoidal structure in
\mathrm{PrCat}
is closed, so
\otimes
preserves colimit in both variables.

To prove the second claim note that, since
\mathscr{E}
is assumed to be complete, an object
F:\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}
belongs to
\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}
if and only if its fibers over identity morphisms are complete (since those are the only invertible morphisms in
\mathscr{E}
). It follows that we have

(6)

\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\mathscr{E}}\cong\underset{f:\theta\rightarrow\mathscr{E}}{\lim}\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta},

and the claim follows by the same argument.
∎

Proposition 2.4
.

Under the equivalence of
A.10
the objects of
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta})
correspond to functors
\mathscr{F}:\widetilde{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
satisfying the following conditions:

a

the restriction of
\mathscr{F}
to
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
satisfies the Segal condition;

b

denote by
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
the full subcategory of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
on morphisms
\theta_{f}\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
with surjective
s
and injective
j
such that
s^{-1}(\mathrm{Im}(i_{e}))\cong[0]
for all except possibly one
i_{e}:c_{k}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
, in the latter case the sole non-trivial fiber is isomorphic to
c_{p}
for some
p
with
k+p\leq n
, then
\mathscr{F}(\theta_{g})\cong 0
unless
\theta_{g}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
;

c

given an object
\theta_{f}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
such that
c_{p}\overset{i_{f}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
is the sole elementary cell sent to a cell of lower dimension by
f
, a morphism
\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{h}\theta_{g}
is sent to an isomorphism by
\mathscr{F}
if the restriction of
h
along
i_{f}
is an identity morphism.

Proof.

For the duration of the proof we will denote by
X
the subcategory of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\widetilde{\Theta}_{n,/\theta})
on
\mathscr{F}
satisfying the conditions of the proposition. Assume
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta_{n,/\theta})
satisfies the Segal condition, we need to show that the corresponding object
\mathrm{DK}(\mathscr{F})\in X
. We have seen in
2.2
that
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})
admits a stratification
T_{\leq k}(\theta)
, by
[
5
, Theorem A]
we can express
\mathscr{F}
as a finite colimit of object lying in the fibers
T_{k}(\theta)
, since all of the conditions are stable under finite colimits we may assume that
\mathscr{F}\in T_{k}(\theta)
, so that

\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{f}\theta)\cong\bigoplus_{c_{k}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}}\mathscr{F}(f\circ i).

Condition
a
is obvious, to prove
b
first assume
\theta_{f}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
and denote by
\theta_{f}\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
its surjective/injective factorization (which exists by
A.3
). If
f
is injective then
\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})
, so assume that it is not and moreover that
c_{k}\overset{i_{e}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
is the sole non-trivial fiber of
s
. By definition we have

(7)

\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong\ker(\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\rightarrow\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j}))\cong\ker(\bigoplus_{c_{k}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}}\mathscr{F}(f\circ i)\rightarrow\bigoplus_{c_{k}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f},\;i\neq i_{e}}\mathscr{F}(f\circ i))\cong\mathscr{F}(f\circ i_{e}).

Finally, if
\dim(\mathrm{Im}(i_{e}))\neq k
, then the preceding calculation shows that
\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong 0
.

Now assume that
\theta_{f}
does not lie in
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
, denote by
\theta_{f}\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
its active/inert factorization. By definition

(8)

\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong\ker(\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\rightarrow\underset{(\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{e}\theta_{g})\in E_{n}(\theta)_{\neq,\theta_{f}/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})),

where the limit is taken over all non-identity morphisms in
E^{\lor}_{n}(\theta)
in the notation of
A.5
. Denote by
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}:E_{n}(\theta)_{\theta_{f}/}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
the functor sending sending
\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{e}\theta_{g}
to
\mathscr{F}(\theta_{g})
and by
i:C\hookrightarrow E_{n}(\theta)_{\theta_{f}/}
the full subcategory on morphisms with target in
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
, we claim that
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}\cong i_{*}i^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
. Indeed, first observe that that
C\cong\{c_{k}\overset{i_{e}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f},\;\dim(f\circ i)<k\}^{\triangleright}
, where the cone point is given by
\theta_{f}\overset{s}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{j}
and to the element
i_{e}
corresponds a surjective morphism
\theta_{f}\overset{s_{e}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{f_{e}}
sending all elementary cells in the fibers of
s
to identities except for
i_{e}
. It follows that we need to prove that for any
\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{e}\theta_{g}

(9)

\mathscr{F}(\theta_{g})\cong\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f_{e,0}})\times_{\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})}...\times_{\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{e_{m}}),

where
e_{0},...e_{m}
is an ordering of the subset of
\{c_{k}\overset{i_{e}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f},\;\dim(f\circ i)<k\}
containing morphisms that factor through
\theta_{g}
. Using the fact that
\mathscr{F}\in T_{k}(\theta)
(
9
) can be rewritten as

\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})\oplus\bigoplus_{0\leq t\leq m}\mathscr{F}(f\circ i_{e_{t}})\cong(\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})\oplus\mathscr{F}(f\circ i_{e,0}))\times_{\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})}...\times_{\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})}(\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})\oplus\mathscr{F}(f\circ i_{e,m})),

which is obvious. It now follows by transitivity of right Kan extension that both terms in the right-hand side of (
8
) are isomorphic to
i_{*}i^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}(\mathrm{id}_{\theta_{f}})
, hence
\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong 0
. The remaining condition
c
now follows from (
7
) since the value
\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})
clearly only depends on
f\circ i_{e}
, hence the morphisms of the kind described in
c
induce isomorphisms as required.

We now need to prove for any
\mathscr{F}\in X
we have
\mathrm{DK}^{\prime}\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})
. Similarly to
2.2
we can define a stratification
T^{\prime}_{\leq k}(\theta)
on
X
such that
\mathscr{F}\in T_{\leq k}(\theta)
if and only if
\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong 0
if
\dim(\theta_{f})>k
, then as before it suffices to assume
\mathscr{F}\in T^{\prime}_{k}(\theta)
for some
k
. By definition it follows that
\mathscr{F}(\theta_{j})\cong\bigoplus_{c_{k}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}}\mathscr{F}(j\circ i)
for injective
j
and for non-injective
\theta_{f}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
with a corresponding morphism
i_{e}:c_{p}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
we have
\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong 0
unless
k=p
. It now follows from this and (
136
) that

\mathrm{DK}^{\prime}\mathscr{F}(\theta_{f})\cong\bigoplus_{c_{k}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}}\mathrm{DK}\mathscr{F}(f\circ i),

which obviously satisfies the Segal condition.
∎

To state the next result we will need some notation that will not be used elsewhere.

Notation 2.5
.

Given
\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta_{k}
and
m\geq 0
denote by
\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta_{k+m}
inductively by setting
\Sigma^{0}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\theta^{\prime}
and
\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}
to be the category with two objects
\{0,1\}
such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}}(0,1)=\Sigma^{m-1}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}
. It is easy to see from this description that any morphism
f:\theta^{\prime}\rightarrow\theta^{\prime\prime}
induces a morphism
\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}f:\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime}\rightarrow\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}\theta^{\prime\prime}
making
\Sigma^{m}_{\theta}
a functor.

Lemma 2.6
.

The category
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
admits the following explicit description: it is a Cartesian fibration over
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
whose fiber over
\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
is the pointed category
K_{j}
with objects given by
*_{j}
as well as objects of the form
(c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},k)
, where
1\leq k\leq n-l
, the morphisms in the fibers are of the following types:

(1)

for any
(i,k)
a morphism
d_{k}:(i,k)\rightarrow(i,k+1)
as well as a morphism
d_{0}:*_{j}\rightarrow(i,0)
for all
i
;

(2)

for any pair
(i,k)
,
(i^{\prime},s)
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
such that there is a factorization
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
of
c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
and
s+r=k+l
a morphism
\gamma^{s,k}_{i_{0}}:(i^{\prime},s)\rightarrow(i,k)
;

(3)

for
(i,k)
identify
\mathrm{Im}(i)
with an object
x
in
\theta^{j}_{i}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{j}}(i\circ i_{-},i\circ i_{+})
, where
i_{\pm}:c_{l-1}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}
are the inert inclusions (or with an object of
\theta_{j}
if
l=0
), denote
\theta_{i}^{j,+}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{i}^{j}}(x,x+1)
and
\theta_{i}^{j,-}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{i}^{j}}(x-1,x)
then for any
k>1
and any surjection
s_{\pm}:\theta_{i}^{j,\pm}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}c_{k-1}
we have a morphism
\delta_{i}^{s_{\pm}}:*_{j}\rightarrow(i,k)

as well as their compositions. For any
(i,k)
we have a relation

(10)

d_{k+1}\circ d_{k}\cong 0.

Additionally, for
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}
as in the case
2
we have

(11)

d_{s}\circ\gamma_{i_{0}}^{s,k}\cong\gamma^{s,k+1}_{i_{0}}\circ d_{k}.

For a composable pair
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{p}
with
k+l=s+r=p+q
we have

(12)

\gamma_{i^{\prime}_{0}\circ i_{0}}^{q,k}\cong\gamma_{i^{\prime}_{0}}^{q,s}\circ\gamma^{s,k}_{i_{0}}.

Finally, for a pair
(c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},k)
and
(c_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},s)
, a factorization
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
of
i
and a surjection
s_{\pm}:\theta_{i^{\prime}}^{j,\pm}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}c_{s-1}
we have

(13)

\gamma^{s,k}_{i_{0}}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}^{s_{\pm}}\cong\delta_{i}^{s^{\prime}_{\pm}},

where
s^{\prime}_{\pm}
is the composition

(14)

\theta_{j,\pm}^{i^{\prime}}\overset{s_{0}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\Sigma_{\theta}^{r-l}\theta^{i}_{j,\pm}\overset{\Sigma_{\theta}^{r-l}s_{\pm}}{\twoheadrightarrow}c_{s+r-l-1}\cong c_{l-1},

where the first morphism is the natural section to the inclusion
\Sigma_{\theta}^{r-l}\theta^{i}_{j,\pm}\hookrightarrow\theta^{i^{\prime}}_{j,\pm}
, the second is induced by
s_{\pm}
and the last equivalence follows since
s+r\geq k+l
.

For any composable pair of injective morphisms
\theta_{j\circ v}\xrightarrow{v}\theta_{j}
the functor
v^{*}:C_{j}\rightarrow C_{j\circ v}
sends
*_{j}
to
*_{j\circ v}
, sends
(i,k)
to
0
if
\mathrm{Im}(i)\nsubseteq\mathrm{Im}(v)
and to the unique
(c_{l}\overset{i^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j\circ v},k)
such that
\mathrm{Im}(i)\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i^{\prime})
otherwise.

Proof.

We first claim that there are no non-trivial morphisms in
M_{n}([0])
between
c_{l}
and
c_{k}
unless
l=k
or
l=k-1
, in which case those are unique. We first claim that there are no non-trivial morphisms
[0]\cong c_{0}\xrightarrow{m}c_{k}
for
k>1
: indeed, by condition

(
2
)

M

we must have
m(0)=1
and also that such morphism lies in
M_{n}([0])
only if
k\leq 1
. For general
l
we again use

(
2
)

M

and the injectivity of
m
to conclude
m(0)=0
and
m(1)=1
, in which case the morphism is uniquely defined by the functor of morphism categories
c_{l-1}\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{c_{l}}(0,1)\rightarrow c_{k-1}
, at which point we conclude by induction. In fact, it is easy to see that such morphism is in fact given by
i_{+}:c_{l}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l+1}
.

To relate this to our situation we first need to relate the objects of
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
to the objects described in the statement of the lemma: to
*_{j}
corresponds the object
\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
, to a pair
(i,k)
corresponds an object
\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}\overset{s_{(i,k)}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
, where
s_{(i,k)}
is the surjective morphism with the sole non-trivial fiber
s_{(i,k)}^{-1}(\mathrm{Im}(i))\cong c_{k}
. Note also that any morphism
\theta_{f_{0}}\xrightarrow{g}\theta_{f_{1}}
induces a commutative diagram

(15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{f_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{f_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{\mathrm{inj}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}

upon taking the surjective/injective factorization of
A.3
, the morphisms in the fiber
K_{j}
correspond to morphisms
g
as above with
g_{\mathrm{inj}}\cong\mathrm{id}
.

Any morphism from
(i,l)
to
(i,k)
in
K_{j}
then must induce a morphism of fibers
c_{l}\rightarrow c_{k}
, and we have seen above that there is a unique such non-identity morphism for
l=k-1
. This also gives the relation (
10
) since the composition of such morphism is necessarily 0 as there are no non-zero morphisms from
c_{k}
to
c_{k+1}
.

Assume now that we have a pair of objects
(c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},k)
and
(c_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},s)
with distinct
i
and
i^{\prime}
, denote by
i^{\prime}_{1}:c_{r+s}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{i^{\prime},s}}
the inclusion of the cell such that
s_{(i^{\prime},s)}\circ i_{0}\cong i^{\prime}
and similarly denote
i_{1}:c_{k+l}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
the corresponding morphism for
i
. A morphism
(i^{\prime},s)\xrightarrow{m}(i,k)
is uniquely determined by the image of the cell
c_{r+s}\overset{i^{\prime}_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{i^{\prime},s}}
, since
m
must also be injective this image must necessarily contain the image of
i_{1}
. Assume first that
l=0
and the inclusion
c_{l}\cong[0]\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}
is the inclusion of the minimal element
i_{-}
, denote by
\theta^{\prime}\overset{u^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
the inclusion of a category with 3 objects such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime}}(0,1)\cong c_{k+l-1}
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime}}(1,2)\cong c_{r-1}
such that the composition
c_{k+l}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{\prime}\overset{u^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
is isomorphic to
i_{1}
and the composition
c_{r}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{\prime}\overset{u^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
to
i^{\prime}
, in this case the image of
c_{r+s}
must necessarily factor as

c_{r+s}\xrightarrow{w}\theta^{\prime}\overset{u^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}},

where
w
is a morphism in
M_{r+s}([0])
, moreover the restriction to morphism categories
w_{1}:c_{r+s-1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime}}(1,2)\cong c_{r-1}
must be the unique surjection. Since
w\in M_{r+s}([0])
we see by

(
3
)

M

that
w_{0}:c_{r+s-1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime}}(0,1)\cong c_{k+l-1}
must be of the form
q\circ s_{1}
for a surjective
s_{1}
and
q\in M_{r+s-1}([0])
. Since
w
must also be injective,
s_{1}
should be an identity morphism, so
w
is uniquely determined by
q:c_{r+s-1}\rightarrow c_{k+l-1}
in
M_{r+s-1}([0])
. We have seen above that such a morphism exists if and only if either
r+s=k+l
or
r+s=k+l-1
, the morphism
\gamma^{s,k}_{i_{0}}
corresponds to the former case, while the latter is given by
d_{k-1}\circ\gamma^{s,k-1}_{i_{0}}
. Finally, if
i_{0}
is (say)
c_{l}\overset{i_{-}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{r}
, then we can consider the morphisms between
(i^{\prime},s)
and
(i,k)
as morphisms between
(i^{\prime},s-l)
and
(i,k-l)
over
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{j}}(c_{l-1}\overset{i_{-}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},c_{l-1}\overset{i_{+}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j})
and apply our previous considerations. The relations (
11
) and (
12
) now follow by construction.

For
3
by passing to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{j}}(c_{l-1}\overset{i_{-}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},c_{l-1}\overset{i_{+}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j})
we may again assume that
l=0
, so
i
has the form
i:[0]\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
. The morphism
*_{j}\rightarrow(i,k)
is uniquely determined by its restriction to
\theta_{i}^{j,\pm}
since its restrictions to all other cells are identities, the image of one of
\theta^{j,-}_{i}
and
\theta^{j,+}_{i}
must contain the image of
i_{1}:c_{k+l}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
, assume it is
\theta^{j,+}_{i}
. In this case the restriction of the morphism to
\theta^{j,+}_{i}
must factor as

\theta_{i}^{j,+}\xrightarrow{w^{\prime}}\theta^{\prime\prime}\overset{u^{\prime\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f_{(i,k)}},

where
w^{\prime}\in M_{n}([0])
and
\theta^{\prime\prime}
is the category with three objects such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}(0,1)\cong\theta_{i}^{j,+}
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime\prime}}(1,2)\cong c_{k-1}
, moreover the restriction to morphism categories
w_{0}:\theta_{i}^{j,+}\rightarrow\theta_{i}^{j,+}
must be identity. It follows that the morphism is uniquely determined by
w_{1}:\theta_{i}^{j,+}\rightarrow c_{k-1}
, this needs to be of the form
v\circ s_{+}
for some surjective
s_{+}
and
v\in M_{k-1}([0])
. We have seen above that there are 2 possible morphisms in
M_{k-1}([0])
with this target, the morphism
\delta_{i}^{s_{+}}
corresponds to
v=\mathrm{id}
, morphisms corresponding to the non-identity morphism
v
are given by
d_{k-1}\circ\delta_{i}^{s^{\prime}_{+}}
for some
s^{\prime}_{+}:\theta_{i}^{j,+}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}c_{k-2}
. The relation (
13
) now follows by construction. This concludes the description of
K_{j}
, it remains to prove that
\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
is a Cartesian fibration over
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
. however this follows immediately from
Lemma

2.7
below.
∎

Lemma 2.7
.

Using the notation of
Lemma

2.6
, assume we have
\theta_{j_{1}}\in\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
, an object
\theta_{f}\in K_{j_{1}}
and an injective morphism
\theta_{j_{0}}\xrightarrow{j}\theta_{j}
, then there exists a pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{j^{*}\theta_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{f}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}

such that
j^{*}\theta_{f}\in K_{j_{0}}
.

Proof.

Note first that the forgetful functor
\Theta_{n,/\theta}\rightarrow\Theta_{n}
preserves pullbacks, so we may assume
\theta\cong[0]
. The problem is trivial for the object
*_{j}\in K_{j_{1}}
corresponding to the identity morphism, so we may assume that
\theta_{f}\cong\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
for some
(c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j_{1}},k)
. Additionally, we may assume that
\mathrm{Im}(i)\subset\mathrm{Im}(j)
, since otherwise the pullback in question is once again an identity. We claim that we may further assume
l=0
. Indeed, assume that
l>0
and that the image of
i
lies in some
\theta^{m}_{j_{0}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{j_{1}}}(m,m+1)
, denote
i^{\prime}
the induced morphism
c_{l-1}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{m}_{j_{1}}
; note that in this case
\theta_{j_{1}}
and
\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
have the same set of objects, so we also denote
\theta^{m}_{f}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}}(m,m+1)
so that we have a surjective morphism
s_{m}:\theta^{m}_{f}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{m}_{j_{1}}
with a sole non-trivial fiber over the image of
i^{\prime}
. Since
\mathrm{Im}(i)\subset\mathrm{Im}(j)
, there is an object
p\in\theta_{j_{0}}
such that
j(p)\leq m<m+1\leq j(p+1)
. Assume we have a morphism
\theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}\theta_{j_{0}}
such that there is an object
q\in\theta^{\prime}
such that
g(q)\leq p<p+1\leq g(q+1)
, which is necessary for factoring the morphism
j\circ g
through
f_{(i,k)}
. Providing a morphism
h:\theta^{\prime}\rightarrow\theta_{f_{(i,k)}}
such that
s\circ h\cong j\circ g
is then equivalent to providing a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.17685pt}{-22.06593pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
g_{q}^{p}:\theta^{\prime}_{q}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta^{\prime}}(q,q+1)\rightarrow\theta^{p}_{j_{0}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{j_{0}}}(p,p+1)
and
j_{p}^{m}:\theta^{p}_{j_{0}}\rightarrow\theta^{m}_{j_{1}}
are the morphisms induced by
g
and
j
respectively on morphism categories. It thus suffices to find a pullback
j_{p}^{m,*}\theta_{f}^{m}
, which is the same kind of problem as the original one, but with
l
replaced by
(l-1)
, continuing in this manner we may assume
l=0
. In this case assume that the image of
i
is the object
x\in\theta_{j_{1}}
and
y\in\theta_{j_{0}}
is the unique object such that
j(y)=x
, denote
i^{\prime}:[0]\overset{\{y\}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j_{0}}
the corresponding inert inclusion. Then it is easy to see that
j^{*}(i,k)\cong(i^{\prime},k)
, concluding the proof.
∎

So far we were working with the stabilization of the larger category
\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta}
of Segal spaces over
\theta
, we will now identify the stabilization of a smaller category
\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta}
of
complete
Segal spaces. For that we will first need the lemma below, which compares the images of a walking
i
-morphism
c_{i}
and a walking invertible
i
-morphism
d_{i}
.

Lemma 2.8
.

Denote by
d_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq n
the
n
-category obtained from the elementary cell
c_{i}
by inverting all morphisms, then we have

\Sigma^{\infty}c_{i}\cong\Sigma^{\infty}d_{i}

in
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})
.

Proof.

Using
2.4
we see that

(16)

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Ch}_{\leq n+1}(\mathrm{Sp}),

where
\mathrm{Ch}_{\leq n+1}(\mathrm{Sp})
denotes the category of chain complexes of spectra of length
(n+1)
. Using the Dold-Kan correspondence of
[
28
]
we also get an isomorphism

(17)

\mathrm{Ch}_{\geq 0}(\mathrm{Sp})\cong\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}),

where
\mathrm{Ch}_{\geq 0}(\mathrm{Sp})
denotes the category of chain complexes of spectra concentrated in non-negative degrees with
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})
. Using the Dold-Kan correspondence of
[
20
, Theorem 1.2.3.7.]
we may further identify

(18)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathbb{N},\mathrm{Sp}).

Under this equivalence to a functor
\mathscr{F}:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
corresponds a string

\underset{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq 0}}{\mathrm{colim}}j_{0}^{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\underset{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq 1}}{\mathrm{colim}}j_{1}^{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow...\rightarrow\underset{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq k}}{\mathrm{colim}}j_{k}^{*}\mathscr{F}\rightarrow...

where
j_{k}:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq k}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
denotes the inclusion of the full subcategory on
[l]
with
l\leq k
. Restricting (
18
) to the subcategory
\mathrm{Ch}_{\leq n+1}(\mathrm{Sp})
we obtain an isomorphism

(19)

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([n],\mathrm{Sp}).

Finally, note that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{colim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{colim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}}

(which commutes since the corresponding diagram of right adjoints obviously does), so it follows that for
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{n}
the object
\Sigma^{\infty}\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})
corresponds to the string

\Sigma^{\infty}|i_{0}^{*}\mathscr{E}|\rightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}|i_{1}^{*}\mathscr{E}|\rightarrow...\rightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}|\mathscr{E}|,

where
i_{k}:\Theta_{k}\hookrightarrow\Theta_{n}
denotes the natural inclusion. The claim now follows since
|i^{*}_{k}c_{n}|\cong|i^{*}_{k}d_{n}|
for all
k
.
∎

The above result essentially shows that stabilization does not make a difference between a category and a groupoid obtained from it by inverting all morphisms; this greatly limits the phenomena it can detect, but from our perspective it also simplifies the description of
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})
.

Proposition 2.9
.

There is an isomorphism

\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})\cong\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}),

where
\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})
denotes the subcategory of functors satisfying the Segal condition.

Proof.

Note that an
n
-fold Segal space
\mathscr{E}\xrightarrow{f}\theta
is complete if and only if for any
j:c_{l}\rightarrow\theta
the
(n-l)
-fold Segal space
f^{-1}(j)
is complete. It follows from this and
2.4
that
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n,/\theta})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n,/\theta})
corresponds to
\mathscr{F}:\widehat{\Theta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
such that for all
j:c_{l}\rightarrow\theta
the chain complex

\mathscr{F}(c_{l}=\joinrel=c_{l},n-l)\xrightarrow{d_{n-l}}...\xrightarrow{d_{0}}\mathscr{F}(*_{j})

(in the notation of
Lemma

2.6
) lies in
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Ch}_{n}
. The subcategory
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Seg}_{n})
corresponds to
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta_{n})
for which
\mathscr{F}(d_{k})\cong\mathscr{F}(c_{0})
for all
k
, however by
Lemma

2.8
this implies
\mathscr{F}(c_{k})\cong\mathscr{F}(c_{0})
, meaning that
\mathscr{F}
is a constant functor, so that
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Cat}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Sp}
. It follows from this and
a
that
\mathscr{F}\cong i_{\mathrm{inj},!}i_{\mathrm{inj}}^{*}\mathscr{F}
, where
i_{\mathrm{inj}}:\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Theta}_{n,/\theta}
is the natural inclusion, and that the restriction
i_{\mathrm{inj}}^{*}\mathscr{F}
satisfies the Segal condition.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

\Theta_{n}
admits pushouts of active morphisms along inert morphisms.

Proof.

n
starting with the case of
\Theta_{1}\cong\Delta

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{18.4324pt}{-10.79794pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
i^{\prime}
sends
[m]
to the subinterval
[i(0),i(0)+m]
and

a^{\prime}(j)=\begin{cases}j&\text{for }j\leq i(0))\\
i(0)+a(j-i(0))&\text{for }i(0)<j\leq i(l)\\
i(0)+m+(j-i(l))&\text{for }j>i(l)\end{cases}.

Indeed, given a pair of morphisms
f:[n]\rightarrow[s]
and
g:[m]\rightarrow[s]
which agree on the image of
[l]
we can form

h:[n+m-l]\rightarrow[s]

by setting
h(i^{\prime}(j))=g(j)
and
h(a^{\prime}(k))=f(k)
, it is easy to see that this definition is consistent since
f
and
g
agree on the image of
i
and that such morphism is unique since all objects of
[n+m-l]
lie in the image of
i^{\prime}
or
a^{\prime}
.

Now, assume we have proved the claim for
\Theta_{N-1}
, assume we are given morphisms
\theta^{\prime}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime\prime}
in
\Theta_{N}
. The object
\theta
is given by the set of points
\{0,1,...,n\}
and objects
\theta_{i}\in\Theta_{N-1}
for
0\leq i\leq n-1
such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta}(i,i+1)\cong\theta_{i}
, the inert morphism
i
is given by an inert morphism
[m]\overset{\overline{i}}{\rightarrowtail}[n]
and inert morphisms
\theta^{\prime}_{s}\overset{i_{s}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{\overline{i}(s)}
, similarly the active morphism
a
is given by an active morphism
[l]\overset{\overline{a}}{\twoheadrightarrow}[m]
together with active morphisms
\theta^{\prime}_{s}\overset{a_{k}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime\prime}_{k}
for
\overline{a}(s)\leq k<\overline{a}(s+1)
. We define the pushout as the object
\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}
as follows: define the underlying interval to be
[n+m-l]
, define
\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}
to be
\theta_{j}
for
j<\overline{i}(0)
, for
\overline{i}(0)+\overline{a}(s)\leq j<\overline{i}(0)+\overline{a}(s+1)
to be given by the pushout

(20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime}_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{\overline{i}(s)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime\prime}_{j-i(0)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{j-i(0)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

in
\Theta_{N-1}
, which was assumed to exist by the inductive hypothesis, and for
j\geq\overline{i}(0)+\overline{a}(l)
by
\theta_{j+m-\overline{a}(l)}
, then
\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}
admits obvious morphisms
i^{\prime}:\theta^{\prime\prime}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
a^{\prime}:\theta\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}
with components given by the appropriate
a^{\prime}_{k}
or
i^{\prime}_{k}
in the notation of (
20
). To prove that this is indeed a pushout note that for
\zeta\in\Theta_{N}
with the underlying interval
[s]
a pair of morphisms
f:\theta\rightarrow\zeta
and
g:\theta^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\zeta
that agree on the image of
\theta^{\prime}
are given by morphisms
\overline{f}:[n]\rightarrow[s]
and
\overline{g}:[m]\rightarrow[s]
together with morphisms
f^{k}_{j}:\theta_{j}\rightarrow\zeta_{k}
for
\overline{f}(j-1)\leq k<\overline{f}(j)
and
g^{k}_{j}:\theta^{\prime\prime}_{j}\rightarrow\zeta_{k}
for
\overline{g}(j-1)\leq k<\overline{g}(j)
such that the following diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime}_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{\overline{i}(j)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta^{\prime\prime}_{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\zeta_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{k}_{i(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{k}_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

commute for
\overline{a}(j)\leq q<\overline{a}(j+1)
and
g(q)\leq k<g(q+1)
. The fact that this induces a unique morphism
h:\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightarrow\zeta
now follows from the definition of
\theta^{\prime\prime\prime}
and the universal property of pushouts (
20
).
∎

Warning 2.11
.

The pushouts described in
lemma

2.10
are not in general preserved by the inclusion
\Theta_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{n}
for
n>1
.

Lemma 2.12
.

For
q\leq l\leq n
and any inert morphism
c_{q}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l}\cong\theta_{f}
over
\theta\in\Theta_{n}
the induced morphism

\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{n,/\theta,\theta_{f}/}\xrightarrow{p_{f}}\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{n,/\theta,\theta_{f\circ i}/}

is a coCartesian fibration, moreover all the fibers
p^{-1}_{f}(a)
over
a:c_{q}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{g}
contain initial objects and the transition functors
p_{f}^{-1}(a)\rightarrow p_{f}^{-1}(a^{\prime})
preserve those initial objects.

Proof.

Given an active morphism
c_{l}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{g}
which restricts to
c_{q}\overset{a^{\prime}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{g\circ i^{\prime}}
and a further active morphism
\theta_{g\circ i^{\prime}}\overset{a_{0}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{h^{\prime}}
we can construct the following commutative diagram

where the left square is a factorization square and the right square is a pushout square, which exists by
Lemma

2.10
. This defines a functor
a_{0,!}:p_{f}^{-1}(a^{\prime})\rightarrow p_{f}^{-1}(a_{0}\circ a^{\prime})
and a morphism
a\rightarrow a_{0,!}(a)
and the universal property of the pushout proves that this morphism is coCartesian.

Observe that
p_{f}^{-1}(\mathrm{id}_{c_{q}})
admits an initial object given by
\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}}
and also that any object
c_{q}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{g}
admits a unique morphism from
\mathrm{id}_{c_{q}}
, it follows that it suffices to prove that that
a_{!}(\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}})
is the initial object of
p_{f}^{-1}(a)
, however note that for any
c_{l}\overset{a^{\prime}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{h}
in
p^{-1}_{f}(a)
we get a unique commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{!}(\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{h}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{0}}

by the universal property of the pushout.
∎

Lemma 2.13
.

Fix
c_{l}\xrightarrow{u}\theta
,
\theta_{g}\xrightarrow{g}\theta
and
c_{l}\overset{j}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{g}
such that
\mathrm{Im}(u)\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\mathrm{Im}(g\circ j)
, denote by
C(g,u,j)
the category with objects given by spans
\theta_{g}\xrightarrow{h}\theta_{f}\overset{i_{0}}{\leftarrowtail}\theta_{u}\cong c_{l}
such that
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
factors through
\mathrm{Im}(h\circ j)\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
and morphisms by commutative diagrams

defined as above but inside the full subcategory

are homotopy equivalent, so it suffices to show contractibility of one of them.

We begin with the case of
C(g,u,j)
, denote by
C^{\mathrm{act}}(g,u,j)
the full subcategory of
C(g,u,j)
on spans of the form
\theta_{g}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{f}\overset{i_{0}}{\leftarrowtail}c_{l}
, we claim that its inclusion admits a right adjoint. Indeed, by the existence of active/inert factorization we can extend any span
\theta_{g}\xrightarrow{h}\theta_{f}\overset{i_{0}}{\leftarrowtail}c_{l}
to a commutative diagram

note also that the dotted line
j_{0}
exists because the image of
i_{0}
lies in the image of
h
by construction, then it is easy to see that
(a,j_{0})
constitutes the required right adjoint. In particular, it follows that
C(g,u,j)
and
C^{\mathrm{act}}(g,u,j)
are homotopy equivalent, so it suffices to show contractibility of
C^{\mathrm{act}}(g,u,j)
.

Recall from
[
7
, Lemma 9.14.]
that, since
\Theta_{n}
is an extendable algebraic pattern by
[
7
, Example 8.15]
and
[
7
, Corollary 9.17.]
, we have an equivalence

(21)

(\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{l,/\theta})_{f/}\cong\underset{c_{i}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}}{\lim}(\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{l,/\theta})_{f\circ i/}

for any
\theta_{f}\xrightarrow{f}\theta
in
\Theta_{l,/\theta}
. Using (
21
) and the definition of
C^{\mathrm{act}}
we see that

(22)

C(g,u,j)^{\mathrm{act}}\cong\underset{c_{i}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{g}}{\lim}C^{\mathrm{act}}_{i},

where
C^{\mathrm{act}}_{i}\cong(\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{l,/\theta})_{g\circ i/}
for
i\neq j
and
C^{\mathrm{act}}_{j}\cong C^{\mathrm{act}}(g\circ j,u,\mathrm{id})
. Our goal is to prove that (
22
) is contractible, we will in fact prove that it admits an initial object. We will do so by induction on the number of cells in
\theta_{g}
, starting with the minimal case of
\theta_{g}\cong c_{l}
.

We will in fact prove a slightly more general claim with
\theta
replaced by
\prod_{t\in T}\theta_{t}
with
\theta_{t}\in\Theta_{n}
and
T
a finite set. This claim will also be demonstrated by induction, this time on
l
, with the case
l=0
being trivial. Assume it has been proven for
(l-1)
, denote

\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(u(0),u(1))\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\prod_{m\in M}\theta^{\prime}_{m}

for some
\theta^{\prime}_{m}\in\Theta_{n-1}
and
\theta^{\prime}_{f}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{f}}(i_{0}(0),i_{0}(1))
. The morphism
g
then induces a morphism

\displaystyle g^{\prime}:c_{l-1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(g(0),g(1))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(g(0),u(0))\times\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(u(0),u(1))\times\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(u(1),g(1))\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\prod_{t}\theta_{t}}(u(0),u(1))\cong\prod_{m}\theta^{\prime}_{m},

where the first morphism is induced by the action of
g
on morphism
(n-1)
-categories, the first isomorphism follows from the definition of
\theta_{t}
, the second morphism is just the projection to the middle term and the last isomorphism follows by definition. Similarly,
c_{l}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{f}
induces
c_{l-1}\overset{i^{\prime}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{\prime}_{f}
, also denote
u^{\prime}
the composition
c_{l-1}\overset{i^{\prime}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta^{\prime}_{f}\xrightarrow{f}\prod_{m}\theta^{\prime}_{m}
. This gives us an object
c_{l-1}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime}_{f}\overset{i^{\prime}_{0}}{\leftarrowtail}c_{l-1}
in
C^{\mathrm{act}}(g^{\prime},u^{\prime},\mathrm{id})
, by inductive assumption this category admits an initial object, denote it by
c_{l-1}\overset{a^{\prime}_{r}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime}_{r}\overset{i^{\prime}_{r}}{\rightarrowtail}c_{l-1}
and denote
\theta^{\prime}_{r}\overset{a^{\prime}_{f}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime}_{f}
the unique morphism in
C^{\mathrm{act}}(g^{\prime},u^{\prime},\mathrm{id})
. Finally, define
\theta_{r}
to have objects
\{0,1,2,3\}
such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{r}}(0,1)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{r}}(2,3)\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}c_{l-1}
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\theta_{r}}(1,2)\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\theta^{\prime}_{r}
, we define the morphism
\theta_{r}\overset{a_{f}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{f}
by sending
1<2
to
i_{0}(0)\leq i_{0}(1)
(and endpoints to endpoints), defining the restriction to
[0,1]
(resp. to
[2,3]
) to be the unique active morphism
c_{l-1}\overset{\mathrm{Hom}}{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\theta_{f}}(0,i_{0}(0))
(resp.
c_{l-1}\overset{\mathrm{Hom}}{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\theta_{f}}(i_{0}(1),a_{f}(3))
) and the restriction to
[1,2]
to be given by the unique morphism
a^{\prime}_{f}:\theta^{\prime}_{r}\overset{}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta^{\prime}_{f}
defined above, it is clear that such a morphism is unique.

Now we need to prove the claim in the general case, assume that
\theta_{g}
is obtained by gluing a
k
-cell to
\theta_{g_{0}}
along a boundary
s
-cell, i.e. assume we have a pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{s}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{g_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

It follows from this and (
22
) that we have a pullback diagram

By induction,
C(g_{0},u,i_{0})
admits an initial object, by
Lemma

2.12
the right vertical morphism in the diagram is a coCartesian fibration whose corresponding functor
(\Theta^{\mathrm{act}}_{l,/\theta})_{g_{0}\circ j_{0}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
lands in the subcategory of categories with initial objects and morphisms preserving them. It follows that its pullback
C(g,u,i_{0})\rightarrow C(g_{0},u,i_{0})
satisfies the same property, it is easy to see from this that it admits an initial object.
∎

Construction 2.14
.

Denote by
C^{\prime}
the total category of the coCartesian fibration over
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
sending
\theta_{j_{0}}\xrightarrow{j_{0}}\theta
to
\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{k,/\theta_{j_{0}}}
and a morphism
j:\theta_{j_{0}}\rightarrow\theta_{j_{1}}
to the functor
j_{!}
sending
\theta_{0}\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j_{0}}
to
\theta_{1}\overset{i_{1}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j_{1}}
appearing in the factorization square

\hbox to182.15pt{\vbox to119.15pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 91.07475pt\lower-59.57649pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Denote by
C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}
the full subcategory of
C^{\prime}
on objects of the form
c_{l}\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
for some
l\leq k
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Denote by
S_{\mathrm{act}}
the subcategory of
\mathrm{TwAr}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})
on morphisms of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j\circ v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j\circ v^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

with
a
active, then

\mathrm{TwAr}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})[S_{\mathrm{act}}^{-1}]\cong C^{\prime},

where the category
C^{\prime}
is defined in
2.14
.

Proof.

We can view
\mathrm{TwAr}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})
as the total category of a coCartesian fibration over
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta}
with fiber
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}
over
\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
and with pushforward functors
v_{!}:\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j\circ v}}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}
given by postcomposition with
v
. Note that all morphisms in
S_{\mathrm{act}}
lie in some fiber
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}
, denote by
S_{j,\mathrm{act}}
the intersection of
S_{\mathrm{act}}
with the fiber over
j
, then it is immediate that the pushforward functors
v_{!}
take morphisms in
S_{j\circ v,\mathrm{act}}
to morphisms in
S_{j,\mathrm{act}}
. It follows that
\mathrm{TwAr}(\Theta^{\mathrm{inj}}_{n,/\theta})
is isomorphic to a coCartesian fibration with fibers
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}[S^{-1}_{j,\mathrm{act}}]
, so it suffices to produce an equivalence between functors

F_{0}:j\mapsto\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}[S^{-1}_{j,\mathrm{act}}]

and

F_{1}:j\mapsto\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}.

For this note that there is a functor
p_{\mathrm{int}}:\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta_{j}}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}
that sends
\theta_{j}\xrightarrow{j}\theta
to
\theta_{i}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta
, where
\theta_{j}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{i}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta
is the active/inert decomposition of
j
. Postcomposing with the natural morphism
\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}[S_{j,\mathrm{act}}^{-1}]
defines a natural transformation between
F_{0}
and
F_{1}
, it remains to show that each of its components is an isomorphism, for that note that
p_{\mathrm{int}}
is the right adjoint to the natural inclusion
\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}\xhookrightarrow{i_{\mathrm{int}}}\Theta^{\mathrm{inj},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}
and the natural transformation
i_{\mathrm{int}}\circ p_{\mathrm{int}}\rightarrow\mathrm{id}
has components given by morphisms
\theta_{j}\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}\theta_{i}
from the active/inert factorization, and so becomes an isomorphism after inverting
S_{j,\mathrm{act}}
.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

Denote by
S
the subcategory of coCartesian morphisms in
C^{\prime}
and set
S_{\mathrm{el}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}S\bigcap C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}
, then we have

\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}[S_{\mathrm{el}}^{-1}])\cong\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{k,/\theta})

Proof.

We will in fact prove that
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}[S_{\mathrm{el}}^{-1}])\cong\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{k,/\theta})
. We can represent a morphism in
C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}
by the following commutative diagram

Note that such a diagram in particular induces an inclusion
\mathrm{Im}(j_{1}\circ i_{1})\overset{i_{j}}{\rightarrowtail}\mathrm{Im}(j_{0}\circ i_{0})
, we define a functor
F:C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{k,/\theta}
by sending
(i_{0},j_{0})
to
\mathrm{Im}(j_{0}\circ i_{0})
and a morphism
j
as in (
23
) to
i_{j}
, we claim that this morphism establishes
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{k,/\theta})
as a localization of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}})
. To prove that it suffices to show that
F^{*}F_{!}\cong\mathrm{id}
as an endofunctor of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{t,/\theta})
, which in turn follows if we prove that
F_{!}F^{*}h_{i}\rightarrow h_{i}
is an isomorphism for any representable presheaf
i
. By untangling the definitions we see that
F_{!}F^{*}h_{i}(i^{\prime})
is the geometric realization of a category
B
with objects given by factorizations
i\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}F((i_{0},j_{0}))\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}i^{\prime}
and morphisms by morphisms
(i_{0},j_{0})\xrightarrow{h}(i_{1},j_{1})
making the obvious diagram commute, we need to prove that
B
is contractible. Denote by
A
the full subcategory of
B
on factorizations of the form
i=\joinrel=F((i,j))\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}i^{\prime}
, we claim that it is cofinal in
B
. For that we must prove that for any
(i_{0},j_{0})
in
B
the category
(i_{0},j_{0})/A
is contractible, we first assume that
(i_{0},j_{0})
is of the form
(c_{l}=\joinrel=c_{l},c_{l}\xrightarrow{j_{1}}\theta)
such that
\mathrm{Im}(i)\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(j_{1})
. Denote by
c_{k}\xrightarrow{j_{1}}\theta
the unique injective morphism such that
\mathrm{Im}(j_{1})=\mathrm{Im}(i)
, then an object of
(\mathrm{id},j)/A
is given by a cospan
\theta_{j_{1}}\cong c_{k}\overset{\widetilde{i}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\overset{a}{\twoheadleftarrow}c_{l}\cong\theta_{j_{0}}
over
\theta
and morphisms are induced by morphisms
\theta_{j^{\prime}}\rightarrow\theta_{j^{\prime\prime}}
making the diagram commute. In other words, it is isomorphic to the category
C^{\prime}(j_{0},j_{1},\mathrm{id})
of
Lemma

2.13
, which is contractible by the conclusion of the lemma. In the general case an object of
(j_{0},i_{0})/A
is given by a diagram (
23
), note that it admits a natural forgetful functor
G:(j_{0},i_{0})/A\rightarrow(j_{0}\circ i_{0},\mathrm{id})/A
obtained by taking the top row of (
23
), we also claim that
G
admits a left adjoint. Indeed, assume we have a cospan
c_{k}\overset{\widetilde{i}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\overset{a}{\twoheadleftarrow}c_{l}
, then we can form a diagram

(24)

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{40.49158pt}{-37.17159pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where the left square is a pushout diagram, which exists by
Lemma

2.10
. That this is a left adjoint follows from the universal property of the pushout, so in particular it induces a homotopy equivalence of categories, which concludes the proof of our claim since we have already shown that
(j_{0}\circ i_{0},\mathrm{id})/A
is contractible. We now need to show that
A
is contractible, however this follows since it contains an initial object given by
(\mathrm{id},c_{k}\widetilde{j}\theta)
, where
\widetilde{j}
is a morphism such that
\mathrm{Im}(\widetilde{i})=\mathrm{Im}(i)
.

We have thus established
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{/\theta})
as a localization of
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}})
, it remains to prove that it is precisely the localization described in the proposition. Note that every object of the form
F^{*}\mathscr{F}
for
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{/\theta})
sends morphisms in
S_{\mathrm{el}}
to isomorphisms (since
F
sends them to identity), so
\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}[S^{-1}_{\mathrm{el}}]
, to prove the converse we must show that for
\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathscr{S}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}[S_{\mathrm{el}}^{-1}])
we have

(25)

\mathscr{G}(h_{j})\cong\mathscr{G}(F^{*}F_{!}h_{(i,j)})

for any representable presheaf
h_{(i,j)}
. By construction we have

F^{*}F_{!}h_{(i,j)}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\Theta^{\mathrm{int}}_{/\theta}}(\mathrm{Im}(j\circ i),\mathrm{Im}(j^{\prime}\circ i^{\prime})).

Note that
\mathscr{G}(c_{l}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j},j)\cong\mathscr{G}(c_{l}=\joinrel=c_{l},j\circ i)
. We claim that

(26)

F^{*}F_{!}h_{(i,j)}\cong\underset{(\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}},j\circ i)\xrightarrow{s}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}{\mathrm{colim}}h_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})},

where the colimit is taken over morphisms
s\in S_{\mathrm{el}}
. Note that this claim immediately implies (
25
) since
\mathscr{G}
sends all
s\in S_{\mathrm{el}}
to isomorphisms so

\mathscr{G}(\underset{(\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}},j\circ i)\xrightarrow{s}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}{\mathrm{colim}}h_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})})\cong\mathscr{G}(\mathrm{id}_{c_{l}},j\circ i)\cong\mathscr{G}(i,j).

However note that the value of the right-hand side of (
26
) on
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})/A
in the notation from earlier in the proof, and we have shown that this category is either empty or contractible and the latter holds if and only if
\mathrm{Im}(j\circ i)\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, which means it is isomorphic to
F^{*}F_{!}h_{(i,j)}
.
∎

Lemma 2.17
.

Denote
\mathscr{I}:C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}}\hookrightarrow C^{\prime}
the natural inclusion, then for
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{el}})
and
\theta_{j\circ i}\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j}
in
C^{\prime}
we have

\mathscr{I}_{!}\mathscr{F}(i,j)\cong\underset{e\overset{i_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j\circ i}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(i\circ i_{0},j).

Proof.

By definition the value
\mathscr{I}_{!}\mathscr{F}(i,j)
is given by the colimit of
\mathscr{F}(\widetilde{i},j^{\prime})
over morphisms

(27)

\hbox to217.1pt{\vbox to124.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 108.54927pt\lower-60.9604pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

Denote by
A
the full subcategory of
((i,j)/\mathscr{I})
on diagrams (
27
) in which
v\cong\mathrm{id}
, to prove our claim it then suffices to show that
A
is a cofinal subcategory. Denote by
v
the morphism represented by (
27
), we need to prove that
(v/A)
is contractible. The objects in that category are given by diagrams

(28)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{e_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i\circ\widehat{i})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j\circ i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{j^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}

with morphisms given by morphisms
e_{0}\overset{\widehat{i}^{\prime}}{\rightarrowtail}e^{\prime}_{0}
making the obvious diagram commute. We need to prove that this category is contractible ,however note that for any inert morphism
e\overset{i^{\prime}_{0}}{\rightarrowtail}\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i)
there is a unique inert morphism
e_{0}\overset{\widehat{i}}{\rightarrowtail}\theta_{j\circ i}
of minimal dimension such that
i^{\prime}_{0}
factors through
\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i\circ\widehat{i})\overset{}{\rightarrowtail}\mathrm{Im}(v\circ i)
, and this defines an initial object of this category.
∎

Construction 2.18
.

Denote by
M_{\theta}
the category
\mathrm{TwAr}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,/\theta})
and by
M^{\mathrm{el}}_{/\theta}
the subcategory of
M_{\theta}
on arrows between elementary objects, denote by
p_{t}:M\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,/\theta}
and
p_{s}:M\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n/\theta}
the projection to target and source respectively, denote
\mathbb{D}^{\theta}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}p_{t,*}p_{s}^{*}:\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\Theta^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,/\theta})
. Also denote
M^{\prime}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}M_{\theta}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
p_{t}:M^{\prime}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int},\mathrm{op}}_{n,/\theta}
and
p_{s}^{\prime}:M^{\prime}\rightarrow\Theta^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,/\theta}
the corresponding projections and
\mathbb{D}^{\theta,^{\prime}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}p_{s}^{{}^{\prime},*}p^{\prime}_{t,!}
. Finally, denote
\mathbb{D}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{el}}
and
\mathbb{D}^{{}^{\prime},\theta}_{\mathrm{el}}
the restrictions to the subcategories of elementary objects.

.

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.58684pt}{-52.71828pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
